#6842. Piece Together the Perfect Pandora Holiday Gift for Her

From a little sparkle to a personal engraving, these pieces make a point of showing you care.

Great gifts prove you’ve been paying attention. They show you know what brings her joy, what she reaches for every day, and what she’d never think to buy herself. For the women who matter most in your life, that kind of thoughtfulness goes a long way. Mom, for example, deserves something that thanks her for a lifetime of being your first call when something goes wrong. Your sister craves a piece that feels unmistakably her. Your significant other needs something that says more than a dozen roses ever could. The trick is finding a gift with a little staying power, something sentimental, meaningful, and made to last. Jewelry does all three.

This is where Pandora comes in. Founded in Copenhagen in 1982, the jewelry brand has grown from a small Danish shop into a global name, known for its hand-finished designs and signature charm bracelets.

To make the search easier, we’ve picked five pieces for five important women: your mom, sister, significant other, best friend, and daughter. For each one, these sparkling options will become part of her daily rotation, a marker of a particular milestone, and a reminder that the person who gave it to her got it right. Consider this your holiday cheat sheet.

Your sister has always had a way of stealing the spotlight. Might as well give her the earrings to match. The set pairs sterling silver hoops with baguette-cut stones and detachable star charms, so she can switch up her look depending on the occasion. This means she gets two ways to wear them, because even after a lifetime of knowing her, you’re always discovering another side to her that’s worth celebrating.

Some things are better together, including the two of you. This pendant necklace brings three connected stars to life in sterling silver and 14k gold plating, giving the mixed-metal design a little extra dimension. It’s a thoughtful way to celebrate the person who makes your world brighter, shares your best adventures, and puts up with your quirks. The last part alone deserves a gift.

She may be growing up, but she’ll always be your little princess. This ring gives that title a stylish upgrade, with a golden chevron silhouette and a tapered baguette-cut stone at its center. Crafted in 14k gold plating, it’s a sweet reminder that she shouldn’t have to wait for a royal wedding to wear a tiara.

A good best-friend gift proves you know her taste almost as well as she does. The Sparkling Baguette Tennis Bracelet makes a strong case for that, with a sleek row of baguette-cut stones set in 14k gold plating. It’s polished enough to dress up a special occasion outfit, easy enough for every day, and just the sort of thing she’d pick out herself. Knowing her that well? That’s the real flex.

This tag pendant in sterling silver can be engraved with a date, a few meaningful words, or initials that form a message that needs no explanation to anyone else. She can wear it on its own or layer it with her favorite necklaces. The best part? You get to decide what it says. Mom has spent years remembering the little things about you. Now it’s your turn to give her something personal.

#6838. Asking for a Friend, Is the Silent Treatment Really That Bad?

Therapists explain why going quiet during conflict can be so damaging.

In a perfect world, we’d all be emotionally mature, communicatively adept lovergirls (gender-neutral, BTW) dating the same. But to the chagrin of the verbal processors and chatterboxes amongst us, some of us wind up with someone who goes silent in the face of strife. If you’ve ever been on the receiving end of the silent treatment and spiraled, this article is for you.

First, quick definition: “The silent treatment is unexplained, open-ended, and intentional withdrawal of connection and communication to control or manipulate the other partner,” Stacy Hubbard, LMFT, an EMDR-certified trauma therapist and certified Gottman therapist and Gottman master trainer based in Ashland, Oregon, tells Cosmopolitan.

Sure, sometimes this looks like blatant ignoring of your questions and bids for connection. But it can also mean withholding positive nonverbal responses (think: smiles, eye contact, affectionate touch) or swapping them with gestures that ooze contempt, like scoffing or eye-rolling, says Kimberly Panganiban, LMFT, a certified Gottman couples therapist based in San Diego, California. And because we live in the age of the smartphone, “it can also mean not responding to text messages,” she adds.

But now for what you’re really wondering: Why the heck does my partner go hush, and is it as bad as it feels? Ahead, relationship therapists explain what's behind the radio silence, what it means for your relationship, and how to get the chit-chat going again.

Bluntly, the silent treatment is neither a healthy nor an effective way to deal with conflict and other hard-to-swallow feelings like disappointment, anger, or hurt. “The silent treatment is concerning anytime—but it becomes especially concerning if it is used regularly,” says Panganiban.

“Being on the receiving end of the silent treatment can be very distressing for the partner on the receiving end of it,” says Panganiban. If you’ve ever begged for so much as a syllable—let alone a full-ass sentence—from someone dead-set on giving you the cold shoulder, you know how true that is.

Suddenly, the person who is supposed to be your confidant and cheerleader, first call and best friend, is as likely to answer your texts or calls as your celebrity crush is to slide into your DMs Adam Levine style with a “Holy fck. Holy fucking fck. That body of yours is absurd.” (IYKYK). With that comes an avalanche of doubt and despair, burying you in questions about where you stand and whether your relationship will weather the storm.

Through an attachment theory lens, the person who should be your secure base suddenly becomes a source of uncertainty. Over time, this can create a sense that your beloved attention, love, and care are conditional, which “will start to impact the level of trust in the relationship,” Panganiban says.

“Trust requires that both partners feel that their partner has their back at all times (even in conflict),” she explains. When one partner withdraws instead of working through the issue, the receiving partner may begin to question if their partner has their best interests at heart.

To top it off, Panganiban says the silent treatment robs a relationship of the chance to reap the bounty of healthy conflict management, including mutual understanding, emotional intimacy, and the chance to come back together even stronger.

The silent treatment works about as well as a rubber with holes in it… so why do people do it? In part, because many of us never learned how to actually resolve conflict.

Few of us took a How to Fight Better class in high school. Instead, we got our so-called “lessons” in conflict resolution from loved ones who relied on ineffective (read: downright harmful) methods to express hurt, get their point across, or meet their needs. Indeed, many adults who give the silent treatment witnessed their parents give it when they were kids, or received it themselves, according to Hubbard.

“When a child is frequently given the silent treatment, they may mimic the behavior of the parent and become the silent partner growing up,” says licensed clinical professional counselor Kristine Cain, LCPC, founder and lead therapist of Full Circle Counseling in Frankfort, Illinois.

Conversely, some children learn that the only way to restore connection is to set their own needs aside and focus all their energy on appeasing the withdrawn parent. As adults, this tendency to prioritize someone else's emotional needs over their own may leave them particularly vulnerable to relationships where they repeatedly receive the silent treatment, she explains.

Not all silence is a calculated, conniving attempt to control your partner like a marionette. Sometimes, it's a sign that someone's nervous system has gone into overdrive.

In a society that teaches us to prioritize production over pleasure, pushing through over slowing down, we aren't exactly taught to tune into our bodies… despite living in them 24/7. As a result, people aren't always aware of the physiological symptoms that signal a need to step away from an argument, Panganiban says.

She explains: When you're fighting with someone you love, your body doesn't necessarily register the disagreement as just another Tuesday-night tiff. “Our partner is the most important person in our life, so when we are in conflict with them, it feels like a threat to our safety and our bodies respond as such,” she says.

When your nervous system interprets a disagreement with a partner as a capital-T threat, “a series of physiological changes begin to occur in your body,” says Panganiban. “Your heart rate speeds up, your body secretes adrenaline and cortisol, and blood flow to the kidneys, gut, and certain parts of the brain shuts off.” (This is known as flooding).

These changes can make it harder to use healthy communication skills, like thinking clearly, listening openly, and responding empathetically. At this point, some people will respond by fighting Jersey Shore season one style, but others will go quiet…

When someone goes quiet during a conflict because their nervous system has gone haywire, it can look a lot like the silent treatment, but it might actually be stonewalling. Popularized by relationship researchers at the Gottman Institute, stonewalling is marked by shutting down and becoming unresponsive during conflict, often because they're physiologically overwhelmed (a.k.a. flooded), says Hubbard.

“Outwardly, stonewalling and the silent treatment may look the same,” she says. But the difference lies in intent. “Stonewalling occurs when someone enters into a state of diffuse physiological arousal and withdraws as an attempt to de-escalate and self-regulate,” says Panganiban. By contrast, the silent treatment may be a deliberate attempt to punish a partner, avoid accountability, or sidestep a difficult conversation, she explains.

That doesn’t mean that stonewalling gets a free pass. “Stonewalling is harmful,” says Hubbard. In fact, it's one of the Four Horsemen, the conflict patterns Gottman researchers identified as predictors of relationship breakdown. (ICYWW: The other three are contempt, criticism, and defensiveness).

Someone giving you the silent treatment may be trying to make you uncomfortable, while someone stonewalling is trying to make themselves comfortable. Whether your partner has gone mum because they're giving you the silent treatment or because they've become so overwhelmed that they're stonewalling you, neither is good news for your relationship.

Still, you don't need to know exactly why your partner is shutting you out to know that their silence is hurting you—and that you want to take steps to help stop it.

If you want to go the distance, Cain says it's worth being brave and naming how their withdrawal affects you.

She offers this formula: “When you go silent, I feel ________ (worthless, abandoned, not good enough, etc.). My _________ (mom, dad, friend, ex, etc.) used to do this and it is very triggering for me. Can we figure out a way to communicate better so that we don't get to that point again?”

Of course, you don't need a history of being given the silent treatment—or to unpack your childhood woes—to justify wanting your partner not to go AWOL on you. So, you could also keep it simple: “When you stop talking to me without explaining why, I feel shut out. I’d like us to work together to figure out a different way to handle those moments.”

The goal isn’t to say The Perfect Thing, but instead to make it clear that their silence isn’t working for you.

While you can’t know someone’s intentions just by tallying their actions, doing so can be a helpful thought exercise that helps you figure out whether or not this is a relationship you actually want long(er) term.

If your partner tends to shrink when a scuttles gets saucy, or has described feeling overwhelmed they may be familiar with flooding. On the other hand, if their silence kicks in any time you set a boundary, express a need, or do something they don't like, it may be their attempt at control.

Hubbard also suggests taking a beat to consider how your partner treats you most of the time. Does your partner regularly dismiss you or make you feel responsible for managing their moods? If so, their silence could be part of a larger pattern of controlling behavior. Conversely, if they tend to shut down during other emotionally intense situations—not just disagreements with you—that could suggest they struggle with flooding more broadly.

So you talk to your partner, and they’re receptive as hell? Hell yeah, brother!

If you and your partner—perhaps with the help of a relationship therapist, wink, wink—determine that physiological overwhelm is part of the picture, Panganiban recommends that you both get familiar with your personal warning signs.

“Recognizing flooding can be tricky, but everyone has different physiological indicators, so it is important that both partners learn what those are,” she says. For some, the giveaway is dry mouth; for others, it's muscle tension; and for many, it's a racing heart. Once you both know the signs, you can make a plan for what to do when they show up, she says.

Technically speaking, a flooding ritual is a strategy for managing flooding before it leads to stonewalling. But even if your partner's silence isn't the result of a nervous system gone haywire, the underlying principle of communicating when they need space and when you'll come back together can keep silence from jet-setting you to Spiral City.

Hubbard suggests picking a single word—essentially, a fighting safeword—that either of you can say to communicate that you need to take a pause, but will reconnect in 20 or 30 minutes. It could also be a nonverbal cue, such as a time-out hand motion, that communicates the same, says Panganiban.

During the pause, the goal is to reduce physiological arousal by doing anything that soothes you, says Panganiban. “It could be deep breathing, going on a walk, engaging in a hobby, watching TV, or anything else that gets your mind off of the conflict and brings your body back to baseline,” she says.

Panganiban says the most important part is returning to the conversation at the agreed-upon time, which she recommends be no more than 24 hours later. As Hubbard explains, “embedded into this ritual is the expectation that both partners will return to the conversation rather than leaving one person waiting indefinitely.”

Of course, even the world's most thoughtful flooding ritual can’t do diddly squat if your partner refuses to participate. And if they keep pulling the silent treatment out of their back pocket even after you’ve told them how much it hurts? Well, perhaps that says it all…

#6836. Erika Felt the “Math Wasn’t Really Mathing” With Blake’s Finances During ‘Love Is Blind’

There was a lot more to their breakup than we saw.

Erika and Blake were one of the many (many!) couples to end Love Is Blind Season 11 without getting married, and apparently there was more to their split than we saw on screen.

The show’s finale gave viewers a brief glimpse at Erika and Blake ending their relationship seemingly due to logistical issues: he wanted to live on a boat and sail around the world, while she wanted to live in a house and start a family. But Erika says there were some other red flags.

“I was like, ‘There’s no way this guy’s broke.’ He’s got a career and not a lot of bills from what I heard. So the math wasn’t really mathing,” she told Tudum. “Those are questions that maybe I was too afraid to ask at the beginning. They’re very sensitive and personal. But I remember him being so cool about [finances] that it almost felt like he did have money in the moment. He wasn’t sweating about it at all.”

She added that “Finances are something that, over time, you start to see some patterns and traits. Like, ‘What credit cards are they using? Are they paying with cash?’”

On top of that, Erika felt like she hadn’t really gotten to experience Blake’s world and didn’t know what she was jumping into. She literally thought to herself, “‘I can’t marry you, I haven’t seen enough of your life,’” adding, “It just started to feel really dangerous to get any closer to the wedding having already been through a divorce.”

Love Is Blind was filmed over a year ago, and if you’re wondering where things stand with these two now...we have spoilers below!

#6835. Under the Surface

Canali gives a behind-the-scenes look at sartorial excellence: the time, precision and mastery that transform raw materials into a jacket made to last

Under the surface, unseen layers lie. They stay there, silent. They don’t demand the spotlight. They don’t seek glory. And yet, like a beating heart, they form the innermost foundation of any real achievement. Where passion, enthusiasm, and discipline meet, the logic of excellence takes shape. Such is the story of Under the Surface, the newest creative collaboration from Canali.

Under the Surface is a look at the unseen architecture of a sartorial jacket by Canali. Every Canali jacket is born from a complex system of about 200 steps, each executed with meticulous care. Around 15 specialists are involved in the process—one that sees more than 520 feet of thread sewn into 22,000 stitches, both machine- and hand-made. Unseen when looking at the finished garment, the process informs each piece’s quality, comfort, shape, and durability.

It’s not just a matter of talent: rather, it’s about staying faithful to one’s goals, pursuing them with dedication even when no one is watching. It’s then that quality truly makes a difference, and the end result becomes the visible manifestation of a long creation process that demands great effort, experience and willpower.

That commitment of excellence unites the sartorial world of Canali and Ethan Hawke, the brand’s new Global Ambassador. The actor, writer, and director has dedicated almost four decades to perfecting his art, with endless, ever-learning curiosity. For their new Fall/Winter 2026 campaign, Canali has decided to capture what goes on behind the scenes. In the film, Hawke pores over his scripts, rehearses lines, refines each motion: a painstaking introspective process made of research, study, and exercise, preceding each of his performances.

The behind-the-scenes of a Canali jacket starts with selecting the finest fabrics, sourced from the best Italian wool mills. Then, each textile is examined and interpreted. While technology now allows extreme precision and control, some assessments are still left to the eye and expertise of human specialists. Even tiny details such as slightly different shades of color or variations in texture can influence the final product.

The fabric is then stabilized via steaming cycles and specific treatments, to ensure its behavior remains consistent over time. Then it’s time for digital mapping, guiding every cutting room decision: here, each of the jacket’s pieces is carefully positioned to also help keep waste to a minimum. It’s the first irreversible step.

The garment’s three-dimensional structure is then shaped upon the canvas. Canali crafts theirs in-house, adapting it to each model’s fit and fabrics. Made from horsehair, plant fibers, and natural reinforcements, the canvas is modeled on custom-made concave shapes, known as Schiena d’Asino (Donkey’s Back), which replicate the chest’s anatomy. The canvas is crucial for determining how a jacket drapes over the body, avoiding stiffness. It’s here that the volume of the chest, the balance of the silhouette and ease of wearing begin to be defined.

Then it’s time to craft the front, lapel, padded shoulder, and the sleeves. From planning to execution, nothing’s left to chance. The tailor observes, corrects, iterates. It’s a patient process, requiring time, knowledge, and skill. Lastly, the various layers are united through basting, stitching, and ironing processes. As these details interact, the garment takes on its structure and sophisticated character.

Something special also takes place behind the scenes with Hawke: day after day, the actor’s preparation subtly transforms his thoughts and behavior until, almost by instinct, he takes on each role’s intimate characteristics.

Time underpins the whole process. After being assembled and pressed, the jacket is allowed to rest, to dissipate the steam and stabilize the cloth. This is how the fabric finds its balance, and the garment gains a sort of memory: the ability to naturally find its shape again, even after having been worn, folded, or packed into a suitcase.

In over 90 years of heritage, Canali has refined their techniques through repetition, experience, and experimentation. Each garment is built upon the time-tested values of care, know-how, precision. The Canali style is not one made of grand gestures, but daily practice, intimate and subtle: the constant research into a shape that lasts. One hero of the Under the Surface project is the vicuña jacket, available exclusively at selected flagship boutiques by Canali.The campaign, and the collaboration between Canali and Ethan Hawke, speak to the art of shaping oneself, guided by an ambition to do things well with patience and self-awareness. Both in tailoring and in cinema, there is so much quiet work that happens behind the scenes to make creativity possible. Discipline and rigour do not constrain creative freedom; they make it possible. For Canali, excellence takes shape through the craftsmanship with which each element is made. For Ethan Hawke, it is the preparation that allows him to approach each role with confidence.And, as the Under The Surface campaign reveals, each of these steps adds something essential to the finished product. In the case of the Canali cashmere and vicuña blazer (the hero style of the FW26 campaign), it is structure, movement, character, durability, and a lightness that can only be achieved through the combination of expertly-chosen materials and complex fine tailoring. The knowledge required to execute this is almost tangible, felt in the quality of the piece.

The finished result only has value because of the strong, unseen structure underneath. It is a story we can all resonate with. Little by little, layer by layer, day by day, we each build ourselves in pursuit of excellence. Our actions create the people we are – even, or especially, when nobody is watching – in the same way that the apparent simplicity of a piece of clothing hides the depth of work required to craft it. This is the essence of Under the Surface.

#6831. The Women Who “Stopped the Wedding”—and Never Looked Back

“Ihad already bought my wedding dress, asked my bridesmaids, and started imagining what was supposed to be one of the happiest days of my life,” 27-year-old Sacha Smith tells me. “I just didn’t know that the relationship I believed I was in wasn’t the reality.”

After discovering her fiancé had been cheating on her for the entirety of their engagement, Smith called off the wedding. She is one of hundreds of women who—as part of a viral TikTok trend—shared their story of coming to their senses and ending their relationship right before a wedding.

In these TikTok videos, women share photos and videos of themselves in their previous relationships—sometimes even trying their wedding dresses on—with the words of Ashe’s viral song “Stop the Wedding!” playing in the background. In the song, Ashe sings, “Even though the table’s set, the guests are waiting, you can burn the dress and run away. Stop the wedding.” The song is catchy, but it’s also a warning cry to women that it’s never too late to save yourself from a lifetime of misery tied to the wrong person.

And it often actually takes something as monumental as an impending wedding to figure out a relationship isn’t working. “When you’ve spent years overriding your own feelings to maintain relationships, it can become really difficult to recognize what you actually want,” Cheryl Groskopf, LMFT, tells Cosmopolitan. “A wedding makes everything feel real and tangible because now you’re facing the possibility of committing to this dynamic for the rest of your life. And sometimes that forces you to confront something you’ve been trying really hard to avoid.”

This was the case for Katherine Woller, 39. There was no cheating partner or big fallout—just a gut feeling she couldn’t ignore. The feeling started the moment her boyfriend got down on one knee. Even with the gorgeous backdrop of Champagne, France, she couldn’t shake the thought that she was making a mistake by saying yes.

It was only two weeks before her wedding when Woller stood in front of the mirror wearing her would-be wedding dress during a fitting, feeling utterly detached. “I felt completely out of control and lost, as if I were watching someone else’s life,” she says. “At that moment, I couldn’t ignore my inner voice any longer and knew it was time to make a big, hard choice.”

Despite the magnitude of an “I shouldn’t be doing this” feeling, a wedding can make it seem impossible to walk away. This is called a sunk cost fallacy. And even though you understand (via its label) that it isn’t true, in the moment, it feels like you’ve put too much in to the relationship to leave it. Therapist Justena Kavanagh, LCSW, explains that once you’re engaged, the deposits, the guest list, and other people’s expectations build momentum. “Stopping can feel harder than going through with it.”

Tishayla Williams was 30 when she was engaged. She had bought the dress, finalized the venue, and was the only woman in her family who wasn’t married or a mother. The pressure was very real.

But when she had been followed out of a cafe by a strange man and called her fiancé crying, his first response had been, “What were you wearing?”

“I told him to come collect his two-carat engagement ring,” she says.

When your gut is screaming at you that you’re with the wrong person, ignoring it feels like abandoning yourself. This is why so many women stop the wedding—and why Ashe wants to remind us that despite our fears of letting others down, it’s always better to walk away.

“I realized that the money spent and the embarrassment of canceling a wedding were nothing compared to the cost of marrying someone who had betrayed my trust,” Smith says. “I’d much rather have a wedding dress I never wore than enter a marriage that was false.”

Women who call off a wedding usually think that facing their family and friends will be the most difficult part. But Smith, Woller, and Williams all agree that their loved ones met them with grace and understanding. Woller even says that confiding in her friends and family led to closer connections and more vulnerability.

“I think the hardest part was accepting that the future I’d imagined wasn’t going to happen,” Smith says. “I think people underestimate how much emotion goes into that. You picture the day, the people who will be there, the life you’ll build afterward, and even the family you might have together.”

And despite knowing that the relationship is wrong or that calling the wedding off is the right decision, it doesn’t make ending the relationship any easier. “Even when you’re certain, you’re still human,” Williams says. “I spent the first few days sad, wondering if I’d walked away from my one chance at a fairy tale.”

In the part of the audio that doesn’t usually make it to the TikTok videos, Ashe sings, “This isn’t cold feet, these aren’t fireworks. The sky is menacing and that’s an iceberg.”

But nerves and wedding day jitters are always advertised to us as a normal part of your big day. How do you tell when it’s something more?

“Cold feet is usually about the commitment itself: the finality, the change in identity, the idea of ‘forever,’” Kavanagh explains. “A bad gut feeling is about the person: how they treat you, whether you feel safe and respected, and patterns you’ve been explaining away.”

Psychologist Karen Stewart, PsyD, explains that anxiety alone isn’t typically proof that something is wrong in the relationship. Instead, if you feel like you can’t trust them, don’t feel emotionally safe with them, or have unresolved patterns of conflict, these are all typically signs that the relationship isn’t sturdy enough to support a marriage. She also emphasizes that foundational issues in a relationship will not magically improve after a wedding. In fact, they’ll typically only get worse.

Some people are just anxious, and it can be hard to trust their gut feelings. But when you analyze where your anxiety is coming from, you can typically pinpoint whether it’s just your body reacting to a huge life choice or something deeper about your relationship.

“Sometimes we know far more than we are willing to admit,” Woller says.

It always comes back to thinking about the connection and compatibility you have with your partner. “If you can picture yourself five years from now, crying in the bathroom at 3 a.m., wondering why the hell you married this person, there’s your answer,” Williams says.

Calling a wedding off can be devastating. You’ve often spent months (or even years) planning a day, telling everyone you know, and picturing a future with this person that you love. Losing all of that at once can feel like something you’ll never get over.

But this is why Ashe sings over and over again that none of it matters. Everything is secondary to your happiness and emotional safety. And as you can see through the countless TikTok videos, deciding to call off a wedding can save your life.

“I genuinely believe it was both the worst and best thing that’s ever happened to me,” Smith says. Since calling off her wedding, she’s traveled extensively, made strides in her career, and learned more about herself than ever before.

Williams feels the same: “I’ve spent the years since investing in myself and my career, and I’ve learned that life doesn’t begin with a proposal or end with a canceled wedding.”

Although Woller regrets how she handled certain things in that season of her life—and is even sorry for the pain she caused her former partner—she’s learned to move on from the guilt and shame she felt in the aftermath of breaking her engagement.

“I still feel 100 percent confident that it was the right decision for both of us, and I am thankful every day that I dared to make such a life-altering choice.”

#6830. The Only 9 Pieces You Need for a Chic Winter Capsule Wardrobe

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Getting dressed is hard enough on an ideal day, but add frigid temps and icy conditions into the mix, and I’m constantly on the verge of canceling my plans to stay in bed all day. As a fashion editor, one thing that I’ve found immensely helpful in this struggle is having a winter capsule wardrobe, replete with versatile staples that lend themselves to mixing and matching. It really takes the guesswork out of my mornings.

In case you can relate, I’ve decided to share my winning formula here. To maintain style while staying warm, you’ll need foundational pieces like a good coat and boots, plus a few winter accessories and, of course, a healthy dose of shearling. You’ll notice this list doesn’t include a pair of pants. This isn’t an invitation to go pants-less this winter, but rather a reflection of the fact that there aren’t any seasonal staples in the category. Utilize your jeans, trousers, or a pair of satin pants for a more holiday-friendly look.

Below, find nine key building blocks for all your wintery outfits and shoppable options that transcend trends. Good luck out there, friends.

Out of all the available outerwear options, I’ve decided that a wool coat is the first and foremost necessity. Every winter, I’m reminded of the importance of stylish jackets that can make or break my cold-weather ensembles. One of these will keep you warm, and if you shop smart, it will last for years.

The entrance to my high school was a long, uphill ramp, and I have many embarrassing memories of near or total wipeouts. I shudder at the thought of walking on an icy sidewalk. So, as an adult, I’ve traded my boat shoes for lug-sole styles (though they would not have been uniform-compliant) that offer more traction. I recommend a weatherproof option that can withstand rain, snow, and ice. As hard as it may be, avoid suede.

Turtlenecks are the heroes of winter layering. Whether you’re bundling up before hitting the slopes, adding warmth under a sweatshirt, or shielding your skin from that scratchy sweater, your winter capsule needs at least one of these bad boys.

I know cashmere can be costly, but it really is worth it. Plus, getting dressed for work during the colder months is so much easier when you have a trusted lineup of sweaters and knit tops.

Vince Boiled Cashmere Turtleneck Pullover

A personal favorite, I confess! I adore a full-length, long-sleeve dress for the winter months. At a time when I can hardly be bothered to change out of pajamas, slipping into a dress can have a transformative effect. And there are so many delectable options to be had.

Everyone has their own opinion on furs—faux or real. Regardless of where you stand, this is the one time of year when you can lean into this look. Here at ELLE, we have a policy against real fur, so you can rest assured that these options are all faux.

Balaclavas, hoods, or ushankas—I’m not picky. Better yet, why not have a few options on hand? By avoiding them to steer clear of hat hair, you’re only hurting yourself. These make such a difference in staying warm.

Be it a scarf coat or a homemade take on Lenny Kravitz’s giant wrap, your arsenal isn’t complete without one.

If you want to do après-ski correctly, you’ll need a bag with a touch of shearling. Options abound this time of year, so feel free to be selective. I’ve rounded up a few favorites here.

#6829. Iowa Governor-Elect Rob Sand Was Also a Prada Model

On November 3, as part of the blue-vote tsunami, Iowa elected Democratic Governor Rob Sand. The 44 year-old graduated with a political science degree from Brown University and a J.D. degree from University of Iowa College of Law; he even got a Truman scholarship and an honors thesis. And his 2026 campaign was centered on anti-corruption, bipartisanship, affordable living, and public education. But there’s more—he’s also a pretty face.

As it turns out, the governor-elect helped pay for his prestigious college degrees by walking runways in Milan and Paris. At six-feet, three inches, the young, aspiring politician modeled in shows for Giuliano Fujiwara and Number (N)ine. He even made it into a Prada runway, Newsweek reports. It was the early 2000s, and he was reportedly signed to none other than IMG Models—though his profile seems to have been taken down since.

For whatever reason, his modeling past is not something the chiseled governor-elect likes to talk about, the New York Times noted, but it sure makes for quite the resume.

On January 19, 2004, Sand even missed the Iowa caucus in Decorah because he was modeling menswear in Milan. “It had to be one or the other,” he told Newsweek. “And I chose to go to Europe.” (Who wouldn’t?) While Sand did not win that time around, look at him now, years later, up at the top.

Sand took Iowa this week while in a sharp navy-blue suit and a sky-blue checkered button-down that matched his eyes. “Thank you, Iowa! I love you, Iowa!” he excitedly said after catwalking up to the podium for his victory speech.

Rob Sand’s team has not responded to Harper’s Bazaar’s request for comment.

#6828. ‘TMZ’ Confirms That [SPOILER] From ‘Love Is Blind’ Season 11 Got Divorced in June

The paperwork reveals exactly when Love Is Blind was filmed.

Only one couple from Love Is Blind Season 11 ended up saying “I do” this season, and it looks like they’re already done. Sarah and John got married in front of their friends and family in the show’s finale, but TMZ just confirmed that—according to Massachusetts court documents—Sarah filed for divorce on June 4.

The official reason? “An irretrievable breakdown of their marriage.” The docs go on to confirm that Sarah asked to split their finances, go back to her former name, and asked a judge “to bar John from restricting her freedom.”

The paperwork also gives us an exact filming date for Love Is Blind amid fan speculation that the show was in production well over a year ago. And...yep! Turns out Sarah and John got married all the way back on March 27, 2025, and then moved into separate homes a little over a year later on April 15, 2026.

So, what went wrong in their relationship? Fans saw them disagree on several major political issues throughout the season, but according to spoilers from Reality Receipts podcaster Rikkii Wise (@storytimewithrikkii), John had an “emotional affair” with an escort during the marriage. Truly yikes.

The exes will presumably get into the specifics during the Love Is Blind reunion, which was reportedly filmed a few weeks ago and airs this weekend on Netflix. The episode technically drops on Sunday, but Netflix is hosting a viewing event on Saturday with a live chat for those of you who want the tea early. Details below.

#6824. An Everyman’s Guide to Wearing a Necklace

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

You already have a watch you like. This is the next step in your jewelry journey.

Men’s jewelry, I’ve learned, can be a slippery slope. I’ve been a watch guy since elementary school, if we’re generous enough to count a dual-sided compass/watch on a carabiner as a watch. In college I added a bracelet. I’ve been a ring guy for over ten years, thanks mostly to being married and partially to getting lunch drunk in Albuquerque, going ring shopping for my wife, and finding a sterling silver bear signet ring that I’ve worn nonstop since. I’ve had an earring for two years, thanks to a quick weekend in Miami and a well-timed Harrison Ford interview clip crossing my algorithm about getting his ear pierced in a Claire’s.

Recently, I’ve been curious about my personal final frontier of men’s jewelry—the necklace. The other listings above are all fairly low entry points, safe experiments in the world of men’s jewelry. The earring may be the exception, but this is 2026; one small silver hoop isn’t that daring, even in the Midwest. A necklace has never really been my thing. I haven’t felt the pull. That is, until this year.

For an unrelated project, I was breaking down some of the signature stylistic calling cards of Robert Redford. And there it was, across paparazzi pictures and movie stills and in memoriam roundups. In almost every photo, Redford has some kind of necklace, usually peeking from a precariously unbuttoned western shirt.

Further study of what we can safely call Hollywood’s golden age of off-duty style shows the same: McQueen and Newman and their contemporaries, swanning around on and off film, always wearing some sort of necklace or chain or pendant.

This is, of course, a common trap: to look at a famously handsome Hollywood star and think, yeah, all that’s separating me from THAT is a necklace. And sure, a good cheekbone and clavicle will get you pretty far in life. There’s no magic amulet here, nothing that will shazam you into leading-man looks. But there are a few lessons on styling from guys whose literal job it was to present themselves favorably to the world. And a few lessons on what necklaces to choose to help get there.

So, I put my professional online shopping style writing gig into overdrive, going deep into the Getty Images archives and confusing Google’s settings by repeatedly searching “Insert Hollywood Icon + shirtless” to glean some jewelry 101 tips from the professionals.

Instagram and (theoretically, since I don’t indulge) TikTok are full of maximalizing style trend chasers. Why wear one necklace when you can wear four? Why two rings when each finger could have two rings on it? Why dip your toes in the water when you can cannonball into the deep end? And sure, there’s some je ne sais quoi about just committing to something fully and completely out of the gate. But there’s also a lot of try-hard to it.

We’re channeling a more reserved, less #sponsoredpost era of dressing here that cares less about link clicks and more about how things look in the real world. So start simple and start with a chain. My favorite, and currently on daily rotation, is the Curb Chain from L.A.-based Bloedstone. Mine is sterling silver and in the inch length, which hits closer to the sternum than the neck. That’s the prescribed Hollywood A-lister way to wear it, but chart your own path if you want to. Miansai has a thicker 2mm Cuban chain that’s great too. And while we’re covering the M’s, Mejuri has one too. To my slight surprise, my home state watchmaker Shinola makes one, and less surprisingly so, Esquire-approved David Yurman has this giant one featuring a marketing campaign with Michael B. Jordan, who is picking up McQueen’s (and subsequently Brosnan’s) torch by adapting The Thomas Crown Affair.

There’s a whole vocabulary on link types and thickness and clasps out there. Don’t worry about that for now. Find something that calls to you and click add to cart. We’re starting slowly here.

Parting note. There are a lot of chains worn over T-shirts these days. To each their own, but the leading man’s move is to keep it under your shirt. If you want to show it off, just ditch the buttons.

A chain is simple, and sure, the greats wore them. But more often than not, they had something dangling from that chain. Medallion, pendant, a bottle opener in Cool Hand Luke. McQueen was often photographed, including in the pages of Esquire, wearing a St. Christopher medallion. Seeing as he’s the patron saint of travelers, St. Christopher medallions are having a bit of a cultural resurgence, partially because of their traditional association as a good-luck charm for travelers. There are some fun enamel ones on eBay, including these surfing-specific ones. Every once in a while, Nashville-based Americana experts Imogene + Willie will pull together a vintage edit of St. Christopher medallions that always sell out in around 24 hours. Speaking of Nashville, if you’re going to go new, go with Yearly Co. The gold jewelry experts have a 14k version of the iconography with a pitch-perfect 24-inch chain. I can attest to the quality—I gave my wife the brand’s diamond huggies and she wears them almost every day. As the grandson of a Protestant minister, I steer away from the saints out of genetic loyalty to Luther’s 95 Theses, but I recently picked up this Perseverance medallion from the brand, inspired by a vintage signet ring. It scratches the McQueen itch with flying colors.

Secondhand gold is also an option. Alloy Market recently came onto my radar, and I’m browsing it daily for myself and gifts for my wife. They’ve got a small but mighty collection of certified men’s items with pendants and medallions you can mix and match. All of the quality without any of the markup.

Don’t limit yourself to just a medallion, though. Get a little funky. Redford did for a while with this turquoise number that somehow looked even cooler hitting under the sternum. Shinola’s got an option there too. Bali-based Salty Dagger has an Odysseus-approved Greek mythology piece. And why not try a locket, like this one from Pattaraphan. Miansai has a great pendant. Get fancy with the Prevaut diamond ring deal. When in doubt, steal from the girls. I got this With Clarity birthstone necklace for my wife that I might take a swing at myself.

Peyote Bird High Desert Sterling Silver Necklace

Shinola 21mm Turquoise Inlay Tag Necklace

From the abbreviated/slightly bastardized Shakespeare: “All the world’s a stage and one man in his time plays many parts.” Jewelry can feel like a risk, even if you’re following some sort of contrived guide to styling and buying it. Sometimes we pretend until we’re comfortable actually being. Redford and McQueen and company were actors. Pretending was their legacy, and they were good at it. Take a leaf from their book and get dressed for the curtain call.

When we wave goodbye to summer and refresh our wardrobes accordingly, it’s often all too easy to abandon color altogether: to reach for blacks, browns, grays. #6813. Meet Levi Alves McConaughey: Matthew McConaughey and Camila Alves’s Son

The 18-year-old is stepping into the spotlight

Is Levi Alves McConaughey Hollywood’s next rising star to know? Matthew McConaughey and Camila Alves’s eldest son turned 18 earlier this year and seems to be following in his father’s footsteps, gradually stepping into his own spotlight.

Over the years, Levi has joined his famous parents on red carpets and at industry events, most recently at last night’s Airbnb.Org Gala in Los Angeles, where he even had his own solo moment on the red carpet. With two acting credits, a runway debut, and several Fashion Week appearances under his belt, he’s beginning to build a public persona of his own. And in the world of celebrity offspring, all signs certainly point to a career in the making for Levi.

Here’s everything to know about the young McConaughey, who’s just getting started.

Matthew McConaughey and Camila Alves McConaughey welcomed their first child, Levi Alves McConaughey, on July 7, 2008. He’s the eldest of the Hollywood couple’s three children. They also share a daughter, Vida (born in 2010), and a son, Livingston (born in 2012).

Over the years, Matthew has spoken about the importance of instilling a strong work ethic in his three children. During a 2020 appearance at The History Channel’s HISTORYTalks, the actor called his kids his “living legacy” and said he taught them the value of hard work by giving them small jobs on his sets.

“I have my kids come out, we put them to work,” the Dazed and Confused star said. “We give them a little cash for catering.”

Levi has also joined his parents in giving back. In 2023, Alves brought Levi and daughter Vida to Refettorio Paris, where they helped cook and serve meals to people in need. Levi later reflected on the experience in an Instagram post, writing, “I got the chance to cook at @refettorioparis for some people that don’t normally get to have a really nice meal. Refettorio Paris serves first class meals to the homeless. They made a great restaurant with high-end chefs to cook—using produce that was going to be thrown away.”

He added, “It was really cool to serve some people that don’t normally get to have it.”

Levi made his acting debut in the 2025 film The Lost Bus, starring opposite his father. The up-and-coming actor plays Shaun McKay, the son of Kevin McKay, who is portrayed by his real-life dad.

But as he tried out for the role. Levi didn’t want his famous last name to give him a leg up. In an interview with People, the father-son duo revealed that Levi auditioned for the role anonymously, with his last name removed so he could earn the part all on his own.

“Can you pull the last name?” Matthew asked the film’s casting director. “She goes, ‘Yeah.’ And I got a call back and she said, ‘Our director Paul Greengrass said, ‘That’s the kid. That’s the son, Levi.’ And then she goes, and ‘I told him that’s actually Matthew’s son in real life.’”

For Levi, the role was an opportunity to explore his interest in acting. “This role wasn’t me going, ‘Oh, I’m going to become an actor,’ It was me saying, ‘I’m really interested in this.’”

Levi has entered the fashion scene on a few occasions, including sitting front row at the Dior Homme Spring 2026 show during Paris Fashion Week.

He also made his official catwalk debut during Milan Fashion Week this season, walking the Vogue World runway.

In 2025, Levi opened up to Interview magazine about his mom, dad, and budding Hollywood career. The teen shared why he idolizes both of his parents: “My dad for the man he is, because of the work he has been able to create, and how he’s handled it all,” he said. “My mom for the woman she is, her patience, and her want to help others.”

When asked if he wanted to be famous, he said, “Famous in a lasting way. I want to continue to build on a legacy that I have the privilege to carry on.”

#6811. My Holiday Wish List Includes One Brand Only

It’s that time of year—when I start dropping not-so-subtle hints to my family and friends about what I want for the holidays. This season, there’s only one thing on my radar—and it comes in a little white box.

A charm, a bracelet, a ring, or a chain—personally, I’m open to any, and all, of the above (just in case anyone was wondering).

The holiday wish list practically writes itself, but to give my nearest and dearest a helping hand, I’ve spelled out exactly what each of them could give me below.

We both know I have exquisite taste in jewelry. What can I say? I inherited it from you. With this stackable diamond ring, I’ll carry that fact with me through all of life’s biggest moments. And with any luck, I’ll finally stop stealing from your jewelry box.

Consider this your reminder that Mom isn’t the only woman in your life who delights in a nice piece of jewelry. This Cuban Chain Necklace is as “Timeless” as you are—and as pretty as I am. It’s the perfect Christmas gift for a father to give his daughter.

You make every holiday more special, and when we’re together, that’s the only gift I need. That being said, I wouldn’t refuse a sparkly little memento commemorating our time together. Buy me something nice and I promise I’ll wear it for our next date night.

When I look back on the most magical holidays of my life, I don’t think of the gifts I opened or the food I ate. I think of you, and the joy on your face as you opened the present I chose for you. The best part of my Christmas is always being your Santa.

Remember when we were in elementary school, wearing our matching BFF necklaces? All these years later, our taste in jewelry has evolved, but one thing remains the same: our love for each other. Let’s upgrade the nostalgic symbol with diamonds this time.

#6808. Novelty Tights Are the Easiest Way to Make Your Fall Outfits More Interesting

From polka dots to leopard print, these outfit ideas make simple fall looks feel fresh.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

If you’re wondering how to get a little more creative with your outfits as the weather cools, look no further than novelty tights. From shimmery to polka dot, leopard print to lace, patterned tights instantly elevate even the simplest look, turning a basic skirt or dress into something with real personality.

The best part is how little effort it takes. A single pair can transform pieces you already own, giving them new life with no shopping spree required. Pair bold prints with understated layers, like a neutral sweater dress or a tailored blazer, so the tights take center stage. Or instead, give your look a more-is-more persona with extra sparkle or shimmer.

If you prefer to ease into the trend, sheer and lace styles offer a subtler way in, adding texture and a hint of drama without overwhelming an outfit. Whether you’re heading to the office, a dinner out, or a casual weekend errand, there’s a pair that fits the mood.

Ahead, we’ve rounded up some inspiration for how to style novelty tights this fall.

This holiday season, give your trusty LBD a fresh perspective with a pair of polka dot tights. We love a sheer burgundy style, which adds a rich pop of color that stands out against black without overpowering it.

Souliers Martinez Casilda leather platform Mary Jane pumps

Tory Burch Bunny Knot Small Leather Shoulder Bag

White or cream lace tights are underrated, if you ask us. We love how they look against a darker palette, such as a navy satin skirt and a matching cozy knit. The contrast feels soft and a little vintage, and the delicate pattern adds texture without stealing focus from the rest of the outfit.

Good American Good American Satin Bodycon Mini Skirt

Sometimes a mix of clashing prints and bright colors just makes sense. Here, a patterned trench coat and black polka dot tights come together with white sling-backs and a bright pink bag, proving that more really can be more when the pieces share a playful spirit.

Cing à Sept Almina Embellished Embroidered Trench Coat

A little (or maybe a lot) of extra sparkle is always welcome, and this all-black outfit gets a glamorous twist with intentional hints of metallics and shimmer, including the embellished tights. It’s the perfect look for date night, dinner with friends, or even a holiday get-together.

Cult Gaia HERA NANO RHINESTONE SHOULDER BAG

Many of us can agree that leopard is a neutral, and that goes for tights too. This ‘fit plays up the wild print and even adds a pop of periwinkle blue.

Susanna Chow Dorothy Leopard Leather Shearling Coat

If you’re already on the hunt for an outfit to close out 2026, a sparkly embellished dress and silver shimmer tights make the perfect combination. Complete your look with even more sparkly accessories and black velvet platforms.

#6807. Ralph Lauren’s 17 Best Outfits of All Time

On Monday night, the Council of Fashion Designers of America crowned Ralph Lauren as its menswear designer of the year—his 12th win since the show’s inaugural ceremony in 1981, minting him as the most decorated designer in CFDA history. The council even has a tradition of creating awards in Lauren’s honor, as they did with the Fashion Legend Award in 2007 and the CFDA Members Salute in 2018.

At age 87, Lauren is the consummate businessman of American fashion, and he’s still producing masterclass-caliber collections that build upon his label’s established codes. In addition to his business savvy and keen eye for tailoring, the designer remains as relevant as ever in 2026 because of something far more ineffable: a deep-rooted sense of personal style. For Lauren, clothes aren’t just a wardrobe; they are identity. His own relationship with getting dressed, from his deep and abiding love of Westernwear to his knack for blending Waspy blazers with rugged workwear and military pieces, has always been at the core of everything he does. Here are some of his best looks from the last 50 years.

Technical hiking gear was a relatively late addition to the RL oeuvre, but the man was wearing hikers in the city long before hipsters discovered Salomons.

Like a skilled party DJ, Ralph Lauren has an uncanny knack for combining unlikely elements—like a yachtsman’s sweater and a pair of worn-in blue jeans—with complete ease.

There’s black-tie, and then there’s RL black-tie. There’s no doubt that Lauren can wear a pair of tuxedo pants and patent leather shoes as well as anyone, but he is much more himself in jeans and cowboy boots.

A two-year stint at Baruch College in Manhattan was the closest Lauren came to the Ivy League, but that didn’t stop him from adopting repp ties, Oxford collar shirts, and other hallmarks of the East Coast elite. The juxtaposition of oversized aviator shades with this solidly trad look is quintessential Ralph.

Think a funky belt can’t be the defining element of an otherwise understated fit? Lauren would respectfully disagree.

Few people will ever look as good in a biker jacket as Ralph Lauren did at age 70. The difference is in the details—the flannel shirt unbuttoned and tucked, the crisp white henley—and the confidence.

Lauren is a master editor, but the magic of his greatest looks is in details like a vest tucked into trousers, a knotted belt, and a pair of precisely rolled sleeves.

Yacht club up top, honkey tonk downstairs. Another Lauren signature.

Ralph Lauren never learned how to sew, but he has an instinctive understanding of tailoring. By blending the stiff silhouettes of Savile Row with the breezy swagger of American ivy style, he created a look that was all his own.

Style is all about understanding the rules before you can break them. Lauren certainly does, and that’s why this ensemble—from the wide-spread collar and slim tie combination to the double-down on stripes—works as well as it does.

There’s perhaps no more challenging look to pull off than “office cowboy,” but Ralph Lauren makes it look easy.

Leather pants and a denim shirt under a tweed jacket is another decidedly advanced maneuver, but (if executed correctly) a potentially powerful one.

In recent decades, Lauren has increasingly swapped his signature cowboy boots for La Sportiva hikers, anointing gorpcore in the process.

Lauren has always revered the look of traditional British tailoring, from safari suits to Savile Row, but he’s just as likely to pair his flannel suits with cashmere turtlenecks and cowboy boots as neckties and Oxfords.

#6804. Carolyn Murphy Steps Into La DoubleJ’s Kaleidoscopic World With a Joyful Collection

The “Sisterhood of Light” capsule turns classic La DoubleJ silhouettes into festive showstoppers.

There was a little kismet in Carolyn Murphy and J.J. Martin finding each other. When they met, Murphy had recently lost her home in the Palisades to the devastating California wildfires, not far from where Martin grew up. That encounter brought them together as friends and now as collaborators, as the pair teamed on a holiday capsule collection, “Sisterhood of Light.”

“She was the dose of joy that I needed,” the model tells ELLE of Martin. “We love a little woo-woo here, a little woo-woo there. But also what I love about J.J. is how grounded she is at the same time. And she’s obviously got great taste.”

While Murphy and Martin instantly hit it off, La DoubleJ’s maximalist, kaleidoscopic world is a departure from Murphy’s own more pared-back style. “I’m kind of a minimalist,” Murphy remembers telling Martin. “I don’t really wear pattern and print.” Martin laughed, and the two began exploring how to fuse their worlds.

The capsule interprets La DoubleJ’s signature pieces, most notably its Swing Dress, through Murphy’s lens. The A-line maxi, which celebrates its 10th anniversary this year and was the brand’s first dress silhouette, gets a luminous update in the Super Swing, a showstopper with embroidered dégradé sequins that shift from silver to gold. That luminous sensibility carries over to the Opera Swing, which features a gilded fil coupé jacquard, and the Mini Supreme Swing, a mod-inspired number embroidered with hibiscus blooms.

To bring the collection to life, Murphy modeled the designs at Villa Tarlarini, where photographer Kate Bellm captured the campaign against lush greenery and picturesque seascapes. Murphy looked to Marisa Berenson and Veruschka, whose influence can be seen in her wavy 1970s hair, for the shoot—an inspiration that spoke to her idea of world-building for the holidays and beyond.

“There’s [an] escape and fantasy with it,” she explains. “Even for the Carolyn dress, I was like, ‘Oh, maybe this is Robin Wright Penn in Princess Bride. Oh, maybe this will be a great wedding dress for somebody.’ And buying me a martini dress with this jacket is so cool. To me, that’s Hollywood.”

The capsule gave Murphy a chance not only to step outside her usual style, but also to find a creative outlet as she rebuilt her home and explored what it means to get dressed now. “I’m a fashionista of the early ’90s, and I love anything that’s classic with a twist,” she says. “I had these style icons back then. Carolyn Bessette—who I know has been super popular of late—and Narciso [Rodriguez] were actually friends of mine. They were my earliest definition of, ‘Oh, that’s what I want to look like, and that’s how I want to dress.’”

Now, the model is defining her own style. “After all these years of being dressed by people and being told what to wear, you get to a place in life where you’re like, ‘Well, I’m going to define my own style.’ I just think I’ve always been pretty consistent about that.”

#6803. Three New Fragrances That Guarantee “You Smell Good” Compliments

Contrapposto is the latest brainchild of a beauty industry hitmaker

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

You know when someone smells so good it stops you in your tracks? I ran into a friend recently and spent our entire conversation trying not to make it obvious how distracted her scent made me. Part of the intrigue was that I couldn’t quite place what I was smelling. It was unmistakably floral, but its elusiveness made it all the more magnetic. She was wearing Melting Flowers by Contrapposto.

In sculpture, contrapposto describes the shift in a figure’s weight that gives the body a sense of natural movement. Italian for “counterpoise,” it’s also the name of Nécessaire, Homecourt, and Into the Gloss co-founder and former Elle and WWD editor Nick Axelrod-Welk’s latest venture.

Founded with his husband, Casey Welk, in 2023, Contrapposto began as an aesthetic dermatology practice in West Hollywood and has since expanded into fine fragrance, with its first collection launching this fall. On the surface, the aesthetics and scent might seem worlds apart. But as Nick explains in our conversation ahead, the move reflects a deliberate vision for what he wants Contrapposto to become.

What really connected the clinic and the product for me was this idea of enhancing rather than transforming: enhancing your skin, the way you smell, and the way you feel, rather than turning you into someone else. That became kind of the battle cry for the brand.

I also loved the challenge of fragrance. It's such a subjective category. There's no before and after, no claims. Either you like it, or you don't. And I saw a white space in luxury fragrance, where the more you spend, the more you're expected to project and have people notice your scent. I wanted to do something different.

I wanted to introduce scents that melt into your skin and enhance the way you smell and feel. Because I think the only reason to put something into the world in 2026 is to truly add pleasure to someone’s life.

They’re really meant to be experienced on skin. All of the fragrances are quite heavy in musks and lactones, which are molecules that are very dynamic on skin, so the fragrances come alive when you wear them. They can smell different from what you might expect on paper, too. Someone might think they’d never wear a floral fragrance, for example, but then try Melting Flowers and discover this creamy, addictive, woody, sandalwood base that makes them reconsider.

I wouldn’t call them subtle skin scents, though. These are big fragrances that project. They just have this ability to impart a coziness that I love in all my fragrances.

I approach everything by asking, “Who are the best people in this field?” When I started thinking about creating fragrance, I took a step back and thought about the launches that had made a real impact on me when I was an editor. The one I still think about is Tom Ford Private Blend. I remember seeing it at Barneys or Bergdorf Goodman in New York. The amber bottles and the olfactive profiles. These dark woods, ouds, and leathers were new to a Western audience at the time, and yet there was something inherently addictive and commercial about the scents. They were likable.

That was the balance I wanted to strike with Contrapposto: something new and unique that doesn’t smell like anything else, but also has this inherent commerciality to it—something so appealing that it invites as many people to the party as possible.

I knew fragrance was its own world, with its own language, and that if I wasn’t the expert, I needed to find the expert. My job was to find the right people and get them excited about what I was trying to create, so we could do it together. This led me to Karyn Khoury, who had been instrumental in developing Private Blend during her time at Estée Lauder. But Karyn had retired in 2019, moved to Florida, and wasn’t exactly looking to be found.

Long story short, I was able to reach her through a mutual connection, and over the course of a year, we slowly got to know each other. I had her smell what I had been working on and eventually convinced her to become an advisor for me.

Karyn also introduced me to Shyamala Maisondieu [a master perfumer at Givaudan], and we immediately clicked. She’s the kind of person who lives with the fragrances she’s creating. She wears them on her own skin and pays attention to how they evolve. While developing Musc Pétille, she texted me from vacation to say it was finally complete because someone stopped her to ask what she was wearing.

I knew Frank from interviewing him for my podcast, Eyewitness Beauty, several years earlier. What I quickly realized is that getting a meeting with these master perfumers is one thing, but getting them genuinely excited about what you’re doing is a much bigger challenge.

There are only so many people at his level, and major fragrance houses have very little bandwidth for smaller, independent brands like mine. Any time someone like Frank spends working on Contrapposto is time he isn’t spending on a major client like Calvin Klein or L’Oréal.

Frank is a genius who is well-known for his woody, spicy scents, so I wanted to challenge him to create a floral. I think I was able to entice him by giving him full creative freedom from the start. I told him to create without budget constraints and see how he could bring his signature DNA into a more traditionally floral fragrance, which ended up becoming Melting Flowers, the first fragrance we finished in the line.

I think trends are really fun, and I love following them. I’m obsessed with TikTok trends, beauty trends, all of it. But I think longevity always comes down to having the best product. If you have the best product, you’ll keep a consumer. Loyalty is made with product, not with brand.

We’ve seen plenty of examples of brands that have spiked and then fallen out of favor because a new, fun, shiny thing comes along. With some products, like Nécessaire’s body wash, I think they create an enormous amount of loyalty because nothing beats it. I still buy it with my own money because I just think it’s the best.

I believe in creating things that are a pleasure to use, so you enjoy the process. They should have some sort of immediate benefit, whether it’s an immediate glow from oxygenation, a cap that clicks in an oddly satisfying way, or the experience of feeling and smelling a product—how it makes your shower smell or how it makes your sweaters smell. Those are the things that keep someone coming back and, I think, make a product timeless.

#6802. A New Marni x Hoka Sneaker Is Almost Here—Here’s What to Know

The Italian fashion house once again brings its unique touch to the athletic brand.

Last year, when Marni and Hoka launched their first collaboration, the two labels came together to bring the world a collection of puffy, chunky, candy-colored takes on the athletic footwear brand’s Bondi sneaker. Naturally, it was a hit among the streetwear set, including the likes of Steph Curry. Of course, a lot can change in the span of a year and a half—and the latest sneaker trends are no exception. In the same way jeans have gone from baggy styles to straight-leg and even skinny jeans, fashion has taken its sneakers from their dad eras to silhouettes that lean a little sleeker. The latest result of these turning tides: The Hoka Clifton Marni.

“Shifting from the maximalist and oversized aesthetic towards a calmer, more essential form made sense with Meryll [Rogge]’s ascension to Marni,” Thomas Cykana, Hoka’s senior director of global collaborations, tells ELLE in an email. “This affords a simple but surprising design that cues premium embellishments, metallic hardware, and unexpected prints over an overtly in-your-face design. This shift enables consumers to both craft their uniform and express their individuality without sacrificing Hoka’s pure performance capabilities.”

Launching globally on November 13, the design is the culmination of form and function. Marni brings its unique design perspective to the technical shoe, not only with the chosen colors—Orange, Black, and White—but also with design-forward touches such as woven heel tabs, windowpane-patterned insoles, and three shoelace options for each colorway.

“Marni has a unique ability to take everyday design cues and make them feel artful and unexpected. That lust for curiosity and imaginative nature is what first drew our team to the label and continues to inspire us,” Cykana shares. “Our debut collection in 2025 showed how much our teams share in creativity and craftsmanship, and that trust led us to our unique spin on the iconic Clifton. The editorial tone they delivered to this project reflects the perfect sensation of everyday and extraordinary at once.”

The Hoka Clifton Marni sneaker will be available for $275 on November 13 at Hoka.com and select global retailers.

#6800. Anne Hathaway and J.Lo Both Love Chocolate Cherry Nails: Here’s How to Wear Them This Fall

The chocolate-brown-meets-cherry-red combo is entirely irresistible.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

At this point, I know all too well that my fall manicure rotation will always include some variation of burgundy nails, chocolate brown nails, and black cherry nails. But what happens when you combine all three? You get chocolate cherry nails, the rich, red-meets-brown manicure that’s been getting a lot of love from Hollywood. Jennifer Lopez has given the moody shade her stamp of approval and Anne Hathaway stepped out wearing a similar cherry cocoa manicure earlier this year.

“Chocolate cherry is one of those wonderfully safe, sophisticated colors that simply goes with everything,” says nail artist Max Estrada. “It’s versatile enough to complement almost any wardrobe, which is precisely why I think celebrities gravitate towards it. It’s glamorous without demanding too much attention, but it certainly knows how to make an entrance.” From short, glossy nails à la Anne Hathaway to longer, dramatic tips inspired by J.Lo, chocolate cherry is proof that sometimes the coolest manicure trends are the simplest ones. Ahead, everything you need to know about getting the look, plus inspiration for your next nail appointment.

Chocolate cherry is the marriage of two fall nail colors: deep cherry red and chocolate brown. Unlike a traditional burgundy, which is more of a winey purple, chocolate cherry is a warmer with a brown undertone. (And it’s pretty dang expensive-looking, I might add.) “It’s incredibly easy to wear without being boring,” says Estrada. “You get the classic elegance of a red manicure mixed with the richness of chocolate brown, creating something that feels expensive, polished and a little mysterious.”

The color is also a perfect transitional shade for fall and winter manicures. As the weather cools off, “we naturally move away from the bright, playful colors of summer towards deeper, warmer tones,” says Estrada. “Chocolate cherry captures that transition perfectly. It has the romance of a deep red and the warmth of autumnal brown, making it an incredibly flattering seasonal color.”

The color looks gorgeous as a simple, solid-color manicure, but there’s also plenty of room to play around with different nail shapes, finishes, and designs. “Personally, I adore a beautifully tapered, long almond nail in chocolate cherry,” says Estrada. “There’s something incredibly vampy, sensual, and sophisticated about that silhouette, especially with an ultra-glossy finish.” He also likes the color with a square nail too.

If you want to get into some nail art, you can go simple or get adventurous with it: “I would also love chocolate cherry with a magnetic velvet cat-eye effect, creating that gorgeous multidimensional movement in the color,” Estrada says. “Or imagine a deep chocolate base with an exaggerated cherry-red French tip.”

Nail prep is always key to a professional-looking mani, especially when you’re working with darker polish, which can be less forgiving. If you’re doing a DIY manicure and you don’t know where to start, here are some tips from Estrada. First, push back your cuticles. “I always recommend gently pushing back the cuticle area, carefully removing excess dead cuticle tissue, refining the nail shape and ensuring every nail looks consistent,” he says.

Next, before applying polish, make sure your nails are clean and dry. “The nail plate should be properly cleansed and prepared before application, while the surrounding skin should be kept beautifully conditioned,” he adds. “Proper preparation means better adhesion, longer wear and a much more luxurious-looking manicure.”

Next, an insider tip: “One of my favorite professional techniques is leaving the tiniest clean margin between the polish and the surrounding skin,” he says. This way your polish is less likely to bleed onto the edges of your finger, which can look messy.

After you’ve applied the polish, he recommends finishing with a cuticle oil for hydration.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmo. She’s covered and tested all things nails, from the coolest new polish colors to cuticle care and nail art trends.

#6797. Everything We Know About The Batman: Part II Thanks to… Heidi Klum?

If you’d told me even a month ago we’d get significant spoilers for The Batman: Part II, the next Robert Pattinson-led Batman movie, in a photo Heidi Klum posted for her annual Halloween costume, I would find that both weirdly specific and entirely unbelievable.

And yet, that looks to be exactly what happened this past weekend when prosthetics supplier The Makeup Armoury posted BTS photos of the work prosthetics expert Mike Marino (who also worked to transform Colin Farrell into The Penguin for the first film and subsequent HBO Max series) was doing for Klum. If you’ve somehow been off-grid for the last few years, you may have missed that the famed supermodel takes Halloween incredibly seriously, frequently donning costumes with extensive makeup and designs that make her unrecognizable. This year was no exception, but it might have gone largely unnoticed had that photo not accidentally revealed clues about the upcoming superhero sequel, which is currently in production with an expected release date of February 18th, 2028.

In the photo, Marino, who provided a helping, ahem, hand in Klum’s costume together for the year, had a number of molds lingering in the background, which have seemingly confirmed that Brian Tyree Henry will play Two-Face, Sebastian Stan will play Victor Zsasz, and that Part II will feature the famed Court of Owls.

Henry’s mold appears to show the Atlanta actor with a disfigured face that mimics the iconic villain. Some, including me, in this very outlet, have, speculated that he might be playing either Dent or Detective Harvey Bullock, a hardened Gotham City cop who frequently works alongside Jim Gordon. But this seems to confirm it. Considering writer-director Matt Reeves has cited Jeph Loeb and Tim Sale’s The Long Halloween as an influence on his version of the Caped Crusader, it’s not ultimately a huge surprise, but perhaps a slight disappointment considering Reeves had stated his intention to move the sequel into uncharted territory by featuring a villain that had “never really been done in a film before.”

Which brings us to Victor Zsasz, who Stan seems to be playing. There was some speculation when he shaved his head upon joining the production he might be playing the character, but the cast looks to confirm the speculation. While the serial killer—who carves tally marks into his body for every person he’s killed—shows up in Batman Begins as a minor enforcer in Carmine Falcone’s crew, he’s got a much larger role in the comic books themselves. In Long Halloween, Zsasz is hired by Falcone to assassinate Dent before he's intercepted by Batman. Might Reeves look to flip the script here and disfigure Henry’s Dent in the process? This interpretation of Zsasz would certainly fit the extremely grounded approach Reeves took in the first installment. But there’s also some confusion here, as Stan was rumored to be playing Harvey when casting first broke and even went so far as to say he’d be playing the role in an interview over the summer. The French publication Le Journal du Dimanche did, however, later retract his statement.

The last accidental spoiler is a big scoop for comic book fans. Simply labeled “Owl 3,” this likely confirms that the Court of Owls will finally make their silver-screen debut. Created in 2012 by writer Scott Snyder and artist Greg Capullo, the Owls are one of the biggest new additions to the Batman canon in recent memory. The Court is a clandestine criminal society made up of Gotham’s wealthiest and most influential citizens, whose power and influence stretch back to the city’s earliest days. They have immeasurable resources and frequently deploy assassins, known as Talons, to do their bidding. This tracks with the story Reeves told in The Batman of a Gotham beset by a history of corruption. Owls are also bats' natural predators; so that, too, is bad news for Battinson.

While this potential leak is exciting and unexpected, it also raises doubts about the identity of Scarlett Johansson’s character, as she was rumored to be playing Harvey’s wife, Gilda Dent. Either way, never in a million lifetimes could we have guessed that Heidi Klum would be the one to spill the beans about one of the most anticipated comic book movies of the next few years.
